HEAD at `J263` — Wave 3 twelfth slice: v5 **QualityDefinition** family (135 LOC C# source). Most complex Wave 3 family so far — THREE distinct wire-contract shifts in one family: (1) Resource shape REDUCED — v5 drops `minSize`/`maxSize`/`preferredSize` (v3 has 7 fields; v5 has 4: id/quality/title/weight); (2) Route map changed — v3 UpdateMany is at PUT `/qualitydefinition/update`, v5 puts it at PUT `/qualitydefinition` (no /update suffix); v3 has GET `/qualitydefinition/limits`, v5 removed that endpoint entirely; (3) Status code differs on UpdateMany — v3 emits 202+body, v5 emits 200+body. Because the Resource shape itself differs (drops 3 fields), all 4 handlers (List/ByID/Update/UpdateMany) are v5-specific — path-aliasing would emit the v3-with-size-fields shape. Custom v5 Resource + own QualityResource inline DTO (v3's `qualityResource` is package-private — duplicated 4 fields with explicit JSON tags rather than exporting v3's). Side-effect deferral: the C# v5 Update controller still calls `UpdateAllSizeLimits` when `model.MinSize/MaxSize/PreferredSize.HasValue`, but since the v5 wire Resource omits those fields, the deserialised model never has them set — branch is DEAD CODE in C# v5; Go-port v5 handlers skip the propagation entirely. 4 new routes wired (1 PUT goes to the bare /qualitydefinition path which conflicts with GET — Go router resolves by method). 8 tests: list-omits-size-fields (canonical reduction guard), ByID success + 404, Update success → 202, Update empty-title → 400, UpdateMany success → 200 (v5 demote from v3's 202), UpdateMany malformed-body → 400. Hard gates clean. v5 surface coverage 15/~50 → 16/~50.

HEAD at `J259` — Wave 3 eighth slice: v5 **Update** family (121 LOC C# source). Single GET endpoint at `/api/v5/update`. Body shape identical to v3 per openapi v5 — same UpdateResource fields (id/version/branch/releaseDate/fileName/url/installed/installedOn/installable/latest/changes/hash), same 200 status via `TypedResults.Ok<List<UpdateResource>>`. HEAD at `J256` — Wave 1 consolidation slice closed: v5 response-rewriting middleware extracted from 3 duplicating packages (`importlist` / `metadata` / `seasonpass`) into a shared `internal/api/v5/internal/v5util/` package. New `v5util` package exports `BufferingWriter` (the http.ResponseWriter recorder) + 4 rewrite-helper variants matching all observed v3→v5 status-code patterns: `RewriteHappyTo204(sourceStatus, h)` (generic, takes source-status as param), `RewriteSuccessTo204(h)` shorthand for 200→204, `RewriteAcceptedTo204(h)` shorthand for 202→204, `RewriteAcceptedToOK(h)` for 202→200-with-body (the ImportList BulkUpdate case where v5 keeps the response array but demotes status). The happy-body predicate matches BOTH `{}` AND empty bodies — handles all three observed v3 emit shapes (200+`{}` from most provider DELETE/test handlers, 200+empty from CustomFilter DELETE, 202+`{}` from SeasonPass). Also exports `CopyHeaders`/`CopyHeadersExcept`. 8 unit tests on v5util covering the four wrapper variants + header preservation + Content-Type stripping. Three consumer packages collapsed: importlist.go drops ~110 LOC of inline middleware; metadata.go drops ~70 LOC; seasonpass.go drops ~85 LOC. Net: −~265 LOC of duplication, +~130 LOC of consolidated shared code with broader test coverage. HEAD at `J250` — Wave 1 deferred sub-arc closed: J237 `POST /api/v3/release/push` indexer + downloadclient name→id resolve. The J237-era port stubbed both resolves with a doc-comment deferral noting "indexer/dc name→id resolution helpers not yet ported; the dispatch still fires correctly using the body's IndexerID + IndexerName." J250 lands the resolves. Two new narrow interfaces on the v3 package: `ReleasePushIndexerLookup` (Get + FindByName) and `ReleasePushDownloadClientLookup` (Get + FindByName); both satisfied by the existing `*indexers.Repo` and `*downloadclients.Repo` via Go structural typing (both already have `FindByName` from prior slices + `Get` via the embedded `repo.Repository[Foo]`). Two new package-private helpers: `resolvePushIndexer(ctx, lookup, id, name)` and `resolvePushDownloadClient(ctx, lookup, id, name)` — each ports C# `IndexerFactory.ResolveIndexer` at `:95-122` (and the equivalent `DownloadClientFactory.ResolveDownloadClient`) verbatim: lookup by id and by name in parallel; if id supplied but row missing → 400 "Indexer with ID 'N' could not be found"; if name supplied but row missing → 400 "Indexer with name 'X' could not be found"; if neither supplied → null return (caller proceeds with body fields verbatim, matching C# null-fallback behaviour); if both matched but row ids differ → 400 "Indexer with name 'X' does not match indexer with ID 'N'"; otherwise prefer byID. Trim whitespace on name input (C# `IsNullOrWhiteSpace` parity). On non-ErrNotFound DB errors → 400 with a generic "lookup failed" failure (the C# code throws which the ApiController converts to 4xx; Go-port uses the [Envelope] uniform shape per J233). Handler signature `ReleasePushHandler` grew from `(decisionMaker, dispatcher, cache)` to `(decisionMaker, dispatcher, cache, indexerLookup, dcLookup)` with both lookups nil-tolerant (when nil the resolver step skips entirely — matches the C# `if indexer == null` early-out branch which logs Debug and proceeds with body fields). host_wiring.go call site updated to pass `indexersRepo + downloadClientsRepo`. After successful resolve, `body.IndexerID + body.Indexer` get overwritten by the repo's canonical values before being baked into the synthetic `parser.ReleaseInfo` (C# `:97-98` mutation); `body.DownloadClientID + body.DownloadClient` similarly. 18 existing push-handler tests updated to pass `nil, nil` for the new params (no resolve behaviour exercised in those paths); 9 new tests cover the resolve: 5 end-to-end through the handler (by-id-only / by-name-only / id-not-found-400 / id-name-mismatch-400 / neither-supplied-null-return) + 4 unit tests on the helper directly (id-found-only-returns-byID / name-found-only-returns-byName / both-match-prefers-byID / name-not-found-validation). Hard gates clean: go build ./... + go vet ./... + go test -race -count=1 ./internal/api/v3/.

HEAD at `J249` — Wave 1 deferred sub-arc closed: J237 `ShouldOverride=true` Subject mutation on `POST /api/v3/release`. The J237-era port stubbed this branch — the comment in `release_write.go` read "Subject mutation requires series.Service.Get + episodes.Service.Get + json-deserialise Quality/Languages, none of which the cache itself carries the deps for. Documented in AUDIT_FIX_BACKLOG.md as a follow-up sub-row to C.1.i-follow-5a." J249 lands those deps. Two new narrow interfaces on the v3 package: `ReleaseOverrideSeriesLookup` (one method: `GetSeries(ctx, id) (*series.Series, error)`) and `ReleaseOverrideEpisodeLookup` (`GetEpisodes(ctx, ids) ([]episodes.Episode, error)`); both nil-tolerant — when the handler receives `ShouldOverride=true` AND either lookup is nil, it returns 503 (the operator's UI sent an override but the server isn't wired to honour it). New `applyShouldOverride(ctx, body, cached, seriesLookup, episodeLookup)` helper handles the override path: validates the required body fields (SeriesId / EpisodeIds / Quality / Languages — matches C# `:86-90 Ensure.That` chain); looks up the series + episodes; decodes Quality (json.RawMessage → quality.Model via the type's own UnmarshalJSON) + Languages ([]language.Language); type-asserts the cached Subject to *decisionengine.Subject; shallow-clones the Subject + the nested ParsedEpisodeInfo (the cached entry is shared in-memory across concurrent requests so in-place mutation would corrupt it); overrides .Series / .Episodes / .ParsedEpisodeInfo.Quality / .Languages on the clone; swaps the Decision's Subject to the clone before dispatching. Preserves everything else verbatim from the cached Subject (Release / SceneMapping / MappedSeasonNumber / EpisodeRequested / DownloadAllowed / SeedConfiguration / CustomFormats / CustomFormatScore / SeriesMatchType / ReleaseSource) — matches the explicit field enumeration in C# `ReleaseController.cs:94-104` "new RemoteEpisode { ... }" clone. Handler signature grew by 2 params; host_wiring.go call site passes seriesSvc + episodesSvc which both already satisfy the narrow interfaces via Go structural typing. 5 existing tests updated to pass nil/nil deps (no override behaviour exercised); 4 new tests cover the override path: happy-path-overrides-and-dispatches (asserts seriesLookup got id=42; episodeLookup got [10,11]; dispatched Subject has overridden Series/Episodes/Languages/Quality; cached ParsedEpisodeInfo pointer NOT mutated in place — clone invariant), missing-required-fields-400 (table-driven across SeriesId/EpisodeIds/Quality/Languages absent), nil-deps-503, lookup-error-500. Hard gates clean: go build / go vet / go test -race on touched packages.

HEAD at `J248` — Wave 1 deferred sub-arc closed: Windows `getActualCasing` impl. The J208 close-time deferral (Windows-only parent-walk casing recovery via Win32 directory enumeration, kept as a no-op on all platforms) is now split into build-tagged files. New `pathcasing_other.go` (`//go:build !windows`) keeps the existing no-op behaviour for POSIX builds — matches C# `PathExtensions.cs:215-218` (`if OsInfo.IsNotWindows` short-circuit). New `pathcasing_windows.go` (`//go:build windows`) ships the real recovery: empty + UNC + leading-backslash paths return verbatim (matches C# `:215-218 StartsWith("\\")`); everything else hands off to `caseFixWalker` with `os.ReadDir` as the live-filesystem binding. Refactored `caseFixWalker(path, readDir)` extracted into the always-on `pathcasing.go` — it carries the recursive parent-walking algorithm ported from C# `GetProperCapitalization` at `:194-211` (root segment ToUpper, per-segment case-insensitive readDir match, input-casing preserved on ReadDir failure or no-match, drive letter uppercased). The injected `readDir` parameter makes the algorithm cross-platform-testable: 5 new tests in `pathcasing_test.go` exercise it via a `fakeReadDir` from a map fixture (recovers on-disk casing, preserves input on missing segment, preserves input on no-match, uppercases root, falls back when ReadDir always errors). Real-filesystem Windows-only tests live in `pathcasing_windows_test.go` (`//go:build windows`) — 5 tests creating tmp dirs with known casing and asserting recovery + drive-letter uppercase + UNC bail. Non-Windows no-op contract pinned by `pathcasing_other_test.go` (`//go:build !windows`). Hard gates clean: native go build/vet/test -race; `GOOS=windows go build` + `go vet` + `go test -c` all compile clean (race omitted for cross-compile since cgo isn't available cross-host). Net change: pathcasing.go +1 helper (`caseFixWalker`), pathcasing.go -1 stub (`getActualCasing` moved out); +3 new files (~95 LOC total) + 5 new portable tests + 5 Windows-only integration tests.

HEAD at `J247` — Cross-tool integration smoke test for the Phase 6.1 shadow-traffic triad landed at `cmd/shadowtraffic-e2e/` (~440 LOC across `doc.go` + `e2e_test.go`; the slice came in over the prompt's "~150 LOC" estimate because the subprocess lifecycle + port allocation + flush-timing scaffolding turned out denser than the test assertions). Two tests, both `-race` clean: **TestTriadComposesEndToEnd** spins up two `httptest.NewServer` fakes (fake C# returning `{"server":"csharp","id":1}` + fake Go returning `{"server":"go","id":1}` — value-only diff to exercise the LOW-severity classification), starts `shadow-traffic-record` fronting the C# fake, drives 3 GET requests through the record's front, SIGTERM record to flush the JSONL log, asserts 3 entries with method+path matching, starts `shadow-traffic-diff` with both fakes wired as primary+secondary, runs `shadow-traffic-replay` against diff's listen addr (replay exits on log-EOF), asserts both fakes saw 3 hits each (proving the dual-dispatch fan-out), waits one `-flush-interval` for diff to land reports on disk, SIGTERMs diff to force final flush, then asserts `*.diffs.jsonl` contains at least one row naming the `server`-field value mismatch. **TestRecordReplayRoundTripsCleanly** is the narrower companion that isolates the record↔replay format contract from the diff binary — replays captured traffic against the SAME backend and asserts the backend sees byte-identical Method/Path/Body shapes both times. New `TestMain` builds all three binaries via `go build` into a per-test-run tmpdir; binaries map keyed by package suffix for clean test access. Helpers: `pickPort` (free TCP port via 0-listen-then-close), `waitListening` (poll-connect with deadline), `stopAndWait` (SIGTERM + 5s grace + kill-fallback), `readJSONL` (FORMAT.md-compliant — skips blank + `#`-comment lines). Both tests honour `-short` (skip on `go test -short ./...`) so the existing fast unit-test sweep isn't affected. Hard gates clean: go build ./..., go vet ./..., go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/shadowtraffic-e2e/... — under 5 seconds total wallclock. **The triad's format contract documented in J245 FORMAT.md is now end-to-end verified rather than asserted per-binary in isolation.** Closes slice (E) — the final autonomous-loop candidate. Autonomous-loop scope drained.

HEAD at `J246` — Phase 6.2 operator smoke runbook landed at `go/PHASE_6_SMOKE.md` (~210 lines markdown). Per scenario: explicit UI steps, expected wire response shape, observable assertions (DB persistence + SignalR fan-out + multi-call ordering), and a PASS / DIVERGE-LOW / DIVERGE-HIGH cross-binary verdict rubric. Includes a clear layered-coverage table explaining what 6.2 catches that 6.1 shadow-traffic + 6.3 nightly contract can't (multi-call UX flow state, DB persistence across restarts, SignalR fan-out — these layers see each individual call in isolation but miss ordering bugs between them). HEAD at `J245` — Shadow-traffic JSONL FORMAT.md landed at `cmd/shadow-traffic-record/FORMAT.md`. Authoritative operator-facing reference for the JSONL log format consumed by all three Phase 6.1 binaries (record writes; replay reads; diff doesn't touch it but chain-composes via replay → diff). Documents: per-field schema (`ts`/`method`/`path`/`headers`/`body`/`status` with their JSON tags + required/optional + meaning); body encoding contract (UTF-8 caveat for non-JSON; gzip in-place decompression per J244 with Content-Encoding stripped when decompression succeeded); single-value-per-key headers map convention; hop-by-hop + API-key + Content-Encoding redaction rules; Status=0 upstream-failure sentinel (and its `omitempty` interaction); `...<truncated>` body-truncation marker semantics (NOT replay-safe — it's an inspection knob); comments + blank lines (for hand-edited logs); examples for 7 entry shapes (plain GET / POST / originally-gzipped POST / API-key-stripped / upstream-unreachable / truncated / hand-edited-no-timestamp); inter-tool role matrix (record writes; replay reads; diff is a live proxy not a log consumer); stability + schema-evolution notes (additive optional fields are backward-compatible; mandatory-field additions break captures and need a version tag). Pure markdown; no Go code change. Closes slice (D). Hard gates clean (build + vet — nothing to test since no code changed).

HEAD at `J244` — Phase 6.1 shadow-traffic-record gains gzip request body decompression. Previously: a client POSTing `Content-Encoding: gzip` JSON resulted in the JSONL log storing the raw gzipped bytes — replay round-trips fine (it sends the same bytes upstream) but operator inspection saw garbage. Now: the recorder decompresses gzipped request bodies before writing them to the log, strips `Content-Encoding` from the captured Headers map (it would lie about the now-plain body), and crucially keeps the upstream-bound payload BYTE-IDENTICAL to what the client sent (semantic transparency — the proxy is invisible to the API contract). New `maybeDecompressForLog(body, encoding, limit)` helper in `writer.go` (~35 LOC): only matches `gzip` (case-insensitive); degrades to `(original, false)` on malformed input rather than panicking; bounded by a configurable decompression-bomb cap (defaults to writer's `maxBodyLen` if set, otherwise 8 MiB) — a few-KB gzip can expand to GiB so the limit is mandatory. `stripHopHeaders` gained a third parameter `dropContentEncoding bool`. `record()` calls `maybeDecompressForLog` and propagates the success flag to `stripHopHeaders`. 6 new tests + 1 test signature update (TestStripHopHeaders gained a third assertion column): helper-level (gzip happy / case-insensitive `gzip`/`GZIP`/`Gzip` / no-encoding + identity-encoding / malformed gzip / bomb-guard above-and-below limit) + end-to-end (gzipped POST decompressed in log + verbatim to upstream / plain POST recorded verbatim / malformed-gzip POST recorded verbatim with Content-Encoding header RETAINED — header is still truthful because the body in the log IS the bytes the client sent). Hard gates clean: go build ./... clean, go vet ./... clean, go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/shadow-traffic-record/... clean (28 tests, was 18 before the slice's 6 net new + 4 byte-eq sanity reinforcements).

HEAD at `J243` — Shared `cmd/internal/shadowtraffic/httputil` package landed. Dedups the byte-identical `singleJoin` + `isHopHeader` helpers that J239/J240/J241 each kept locally in `cmd/shadow-traffic-{diff,replay,record}/`. New package at `cmd/internal/shadowtraffic/httputil/` (intentional placement under `cmd/internal/` rather than the natural-sounding `cmd/shadowtraffic/internal/` because Go's `internal/` rule scopes import to the parent of `internal/`, and the three binaries are NOT under a hypothetical `cmd/shadowtraffic/...` subtree — they're siblings at `cmd/shadow-traffic-*`). Exports `SingleJoin(base, req string) string` + `IsHopHeader(h string) bool` with identical behaviour to the in-tree helpers. Each of the three binaries' `proxy.go` (and `record/writer.go` for `stripHopHeaders`) now imports `sonarr.tv/sonarr/cmd/internal/shadowtraffic/httputil` and calls `httputil.SingleJoin` / `httputil.IsHopHeader`. Local definitions deleted. Existing duplicate `TestSingleJoin` blocks in `diff/proxy_test.go` + `record/proxy_test.go` removed; a single consolidated test now lives at the shared package alongside an `IsHopHeader` test matrix (10 hop cases + 8 non-hop cases including the substring-trap case `X-Connection-Hint`). Net diff: +73 LOC shared package + ~-58 LOC across the three binaries → ~+15 LOC net (the helper tests are more comprehensive than the prior 4-case tables). Hard gates clean (go build ./..., go vet on all 4 touched packages, go test -race -count=1 on all 4 touched packages). The three binaries remain independently buildable; the only new symbol they share is the package import path.

HEAD at `J242` — Phase 6.3 nightly contract-suite CI workflow landed at `.github/workflows/nightly-contract.yml`. Runs on `cron: "0 3 * * *"` (03:00 UTC daily) + `workflow_dispatch` for manual debug. 4 jobs: **preflight** (gates on CONTRACT_STAGING_BASE_GO / CONTRACT_STAGING_BASE_CSHARP repo secrets — no-op when unset, typical fork case), **go-staging** (replays contract corpus against the Go-port staging deploy, gates on `CONTRACT_DRIFT_BASELINE=9` per the J217-era baseline established in build_go.yml; emits step-summary with drift count + baseline; uploads replay log artifact 14-day retention), **csharp-staging** (replays against the canonical C# staging — does NOT gate on baseline since C# IS the canonical; surfaces drift as a "golden corpus stale relative to live C#" signal for re-recording), **diff-summary** (cross-backend table with Go drift / C# drift / delta — clearly labels whether Go-port is at-or-ahead of C# for corpus-covered endpoints, or whether Go has N more drifting endpoints than C#). Uses the same `-run TestReplay -v | tee | grep "contract drift" || true` extraction pattern as the existing build_go.yml contract-replay job — known-working hack that surfaces per-entry drift count without making `go test` exit code drive the gate. The override_baseline workflow_dispatch input lets the operator one-shot a different baseline for debugging. Concurrency group prevents nightly overlap if a prior run is still draining (cancel-in-progress: false — finish what you started before launching the next). Phase 6.3 is "workflow YAML only, no code change" per PHASE_6_SCOPE.md and that's exactly what landed. Gated on secrets means this is **safe to merge today** even before staging URLs exist — preflight skips cleanly.

HEAD at `J241` — Phase 6.1 tee/recorder tool landed. New `cmd/shadow-traffic-record/` binary (~380 LOC source + ~360 LOC tests). Single-upstream reverse-proxy that tees inbound requests into a J240-compatible JSONL log file while forwarding each request to the configured backend. **Phase 6.1 toolkit triad now complete**: J241 captures → J240 replays → J239 dual-dispatches + diffs. Architecture: async LogWriter with bounded channel between request hot path and disk drain goroutine; non-blocking Enqueue with drop-counter when buffer full (best-effort capture under burst load — request latency never blocked on disk I/O); periodic buffered-writer flush (default 2s); optional per-request body length cap (default 256KiB) with `...<truncated>` marker for replay tooling; optional `-drop-api-keys` strips X-Api-Key + Authorization from captured records (default ON — captured logs are routinely emailed/Slack'd/checked-in, so default-deny on credential leakage); large response bodies streamed via io.Copy (not buffered); upstream-failure records with Status=0 sentinel; `-sync-on-close` fsync at shutdown. 14 tests including GET happy path (verifies path+query+timestamp+status), POST body verbatim capture (upstream echo assert), API key + Authorization stripped from log but PRESERVED upstream-bound (security boundary check), upstream-down → 502 to client + Status=0 record, hop-by-hop headers stripped (Connection drops, X-Custom preserved), queue-full drop-counter (100 entries → queue size 2 → some written + rest dropped + sum=100), body truncation with `<truncated>` suffix, Close idempotency + Enqueue-after-close returns false, append-mode (two open-write-close cycles → 2 entries in file), proxy stats increment, singleJoin + stripHopHeaders helpers, WriterStats.String, 1MB large-response streaming. Race-clean. Operator can now point Sonarr UI at this binary (`-listen :9001`, `-upstream http://localhost:8989`), drive normal workflow, get a JSONL log ready for J240 replay.

HEAD at `J240` — Phase 6.1 production-log replay helper landed. New `cmd/shadow-traffic-replay/` binary (~360 LOC source + ~440 LOC tests). Reads a JSONL request log streaming (4MiB max line — big POST bodies for manualimport / customformat-bulk fit); replays each entry against a target URL (typically the J239 shadow-traffic-diff listen addr). Pacing: `-speed 1.0` preserves recorded inter-entry timestamps, `0` no-throttle, `10.0` fast-forward; `-concurrency N` fans out across worker pool (disables timestamp pacing). Filters: `-method GET,POST` allowlist, `-path` prefix allowlist (CSV), `-status 200,4xx,503` (codes + classes). Per-request timeout via `-timeout`. Periodic stats lines + final summary (sent/filtered/errors/2xx/4xx/5xx/other counts + avg latency + rps). Atomic counters → race-clean. SIGINT/SIGTERM cancels the run mid-stream. Reads support comments (`#` lines) and blank lines for hand-edited logs. 18 tests covering log parse (happy, malformed, missing-required), filter (method/path/status/empty-passes-all), ParseStatusFilter (mixed codes+classes, bad-token, sub-100 reject), statusClassMatch (boundaries 199/200/299/300), replayer (dispatches each entry, preserves query string, respects filter, paces by timestamp at 2x speed within ±50ms tolerance, Speed=0 no-throttle near-instant, concurrency 5 actually parallelises, ctx cancel breaks long pacing wait, error counter on unreachable target, status bucket classification 2xx/4xx/5xx/other), OpenLogFile, splitCSV, StatsSnapshot.String. Hard gates clean. Together with J239 the operator has a complete Phase 6.1 toolkit: capture a request log somewhere (real Sonarr install, manual scripting), feed it to shadow-traffic-replay, point it at shadow-traffic-diff's listen addr, get a daily diff report.

HEAD at `J239` — Phase 6.1 shadow-traffic harness skeleton landed (Phase 6 prep begins). New `cmd/shadow-traffic-diff/` binary (~600 LOC source + ~370 LOC tests) accepts an incoming HTTP request stream, dispatches each request in parallel to two upstream backends (primary = canonical C# Sonarr, secondary = Go port), streams the primary's response back to the caller, then diffs the buffered secondary body against the primary post-redaction via the existing `internal/normalize` package. Daily diff reports written to disk grouped by severity (high/low) — high for status mismatch / missing field / type mismatch / secondary down, low for pure value diffs. OK events aggregated into a separate `*.ok-summary.json` counter so byte-equal requests don't pollute the per-event log. **Refactor**: moved `test/contract/internal/normalize/` → `internal/normalize/` so both the test/contract tree and the new `cmd/` binary can import it (was `internal` to `test/contract/`, blocking cmd/ imports). All 4 prior callsites updated. 14 tests including end-to-end through-front-proxy tests with both backends agreeing, status mismatch surfacing, value diffs as low-sev, missing fields as high-sev, secondary down as high-sev, primary down → 502 to client, POST body replayed verbatim to both backends, redacted-field-diff suppressed. Race-clean. Full build green. Existing contract suite green at the new normalize import path. Default `RedactConfig` covers the most common environmental noise (system/task lastDuration/nextExecution, command queued/started/ended/duration, log timestamps, system/status version/buildTime/runtimeVersion, diskspace freeSpace/totalSpace). Operator can override via `-redact-config` JSON file. Per the Phase 6 scope doc: this binary is the Phase 6.1 deliverable and can develop in parallel with staging-host provisioning — no infra needed yet.

- **J244** — gzip request body decompression on shadow-traffic-record (slice (B) from the autonomous-loop candidate list). Gzipped Content-Encoding request bodies now appear DECOMPRESSED in the JSONL log (operator-readable JSON instead of gzip garbage); upstream still receives the byte-identical original payload (semantic transparency). New `maybeDecompressForLog` helper (gzip-only, bomb-bounded via 8 MiB default cap or writer's `maxBodyLen`, malformed-input safe → degrades to verbatim logging). `stripHopHeaders` extended with `dropContentEncoding` parameter; recorder drops the header from the captured map only when decompression actually succeeded (malformed-gzip retains the header — body in log IS what client sent, header is still truthful). 6 new tests + 1 signature update. Hard gates clean.

Shared tests cover 8 SingleJoin cases (including 3 new edge cases over the originals: empty req, both empty, slash base with empty req) + 18 IsHopHeader cases (10 hop including 3 case-variants on `Connection`, 8 non-hop including the `X-Connection-Hint` substring-trap). Package location: `cmd/internal/shadowtraffic/httputil/` (not the prompt-suggested `cmd/shadowtraffic/internal/httputil/` — Go internal-package rules require the `internal/` parent be an ancestor of all consumers; the three binaries are siblings of `cmd/internal/`, not under `cmd/shadowtraffic/`). Runs on cron "0 3 * * *" + workflow_dispatch with override_baseline input. Uses the same drift-count extraction pattern as build_go.yml's existing contract-replay job — known-working. Concurrency group prevents nightly overlap. Phase 6.3 is "workflow YAML only" per PHASE_6_SCOPE.md and that's exactly what landed.

- **J241** — Phase 6.1 tee/recorder tool (`cmd/shadow-traffic-record/`). Single-upstream reverse-proxy that tees inbound traffic into a J240-compatible JSONL log file while forwarding each request to the backend. Async LogWriter with bounded queue + drop-counter (non-blocking Enqueue — best-effort under burst load); periodic flush (2s default); per-request body cap (256KiB default) with `...<truncated>` marker; `-drop-api-keys` default ON strips X-Api-Key + Authorization from log but preserves them upstream-bound; large bodies io.Copy-streamed (not buffered); upstream-failure records with Status=0; `-sync-on-close` fsync. 14 tests including GET happy path, POST body verbatim, API-key stripped from log but kept upstream-bound, upstream-down → 502 + Status=0 record, hop-by-hop headers stripped, queue-full drop-counter, body truncation marker, Close idempotency, append-mode across two open-write-close cycles, large-response streaming (1MB). Race-clean. **Phase 6.1 toolkit triad now complete**: J241 capture → J240 replay → J239 diff.

- **J240** — Phase 6.1 production-log replay helper (`cmd/shadow-traffic-replay/`). Companion binary to J239's harness. Reads JSONL request logs streaming (4MiB line max for big POST bodies); replays against a target URL. Pacing: `-speed N` multiplier (1.0=real-time, 0=no-throttle, 10.0=fast-forward) preserves recorded inter-entry timestamps via prev-Timestamp gap; `-concurrency N` worker pool (disables timestamp pacing for serial-order destruction). Filters by method (CSV), path-prefix (CSV), status (codes + classes like "4xx" mixed). Per-request timeout, periodic stats lines + final summary, SIGINT cancels mid-run. Comments (`#`) and blank lines in logs supported. 18 tests including timestamp-paced 2x replay within ±50ms tolerance, concurrent dispatch (max-in-flight assertion), ctx cancel mid-pacing-wait, error counter on dead target, status bucket classification. Race-clean. Together with J239 the operator has a Phase 6.1 toolkit complete — capture log → shadow-traffic-replay → shadow-traffic-diff listen → daily diff report.

- **J239** — Phase 6.1 shadow-traffic harness skeleton (`cmd/shadow-traffic-diff/`). Reverse-proxy binary that dispatches each incoming request to C# + Go backends in parallel, returns primary's response, diffs secondary's body post-redaction via `internal/normalize`, writes daily diff reports grouped by severity (high/low) + OK aggregate. **Refactor**: moved `test/contract/internal/normalize/` → `internal/normalize/` (4 import sites updated) so cmd/ can import it. 14 tests including end-to-end-through-front-proxy paths covering byte-equal-no-diff, status mismatch HIGH, value diff LOW, missing field HIGH, secondary-down HIGH, primary-down 502-to-client, POST body replayed verbatim, redacted-field-diff suppressed. Race-clean. Full build green. Existing contract suite still passes at the new normalize path. Default RedactConfig covers system/task lastDuration, command queued/started/ended, log timestamps, system/status build fields, diskspace free/total. Operator overrides via `-redact-config` JSON. **Phase 5 → Phase 6 pivot complete.** Phase 6 entrance is NOT gated on this — it can develop in parallel with staging-host provisioning. The smoke runbook (Phase 6.2) is operator-driven and not autonomous-loop work; the nightly contract-suite CI (Phase 6.3) is a workflow file change not a code change.

Two narrow interfaces (`ManualImportSeriesLookup` for folder-path-based series resolution, `ManualImportEpisodeLookup` for explicit-EpisodeIDs path). New `manualImportReleaseTypeFor` helper maps `*parser.ParsedEpisodeInfo` shape to wire-format ReleaseType string (`seasonPack`/`multiEpisode`/`singleEpisode`/`unknown`) via `CamelCaseEnumName` (matches C# `src/Sonarr.Api.V5/openapi.json` `ReleaseType` enum schema). Per-id pipeline: pending lookup → JSON deserialise to `parser.ReleaseInfo` + `parser.ParsedEpisodeInfo` → series resolve → minimal `*decisionengine.Subject` (with `DownloadAllowed=true` to clear isQualified) → `ProcessDecisions` dispatch. 404 short-circuits bulk on first miss; malformed-body 400; empty-ids 200 no-dispatch (matches C# zero-iteration semantics). 3 narrow interfaces defined in v3 package (PendingReleaseLookup, QueueGrabSeriesLookup, QueueGrabDispatcher) — each satisfied by existing services via Go structural typing. - **J233** — Validation envelope policy (audit row C.1.i-follow-2 closed). Documented C#'s 4 envelope shapes from J228 reference + per-path decisions. Go-port deliberately keeps single [Envelope] shape across all 4xx/5xx rather than mirroring C#'s framework-incidental mix (RFC 9110 problem-details on `[ApiController]` paths + legacy `{message, content}` on bulk-empty-ids + null-deref-bug 500s). Rationale: consistent single parser handles every Go-port error response; C#'s mix is ASP.NET Core defaults, not deliberate. Pinned via `internal/httperr/httperr.go` package doc-comment block + new `TestPolicy_DoesNotEmitRFC9110ProblemDetails` regression-guard test covering BadRequest/NotFound/Conflict. WRITE+READ side (404 read envelopes + 4xx write envelopes both covered). No production code change. Hard gates clean. 1 of 5 follow-up rows remains.

- **J225** — v5 AutoTagging controller port (audit row C.1.e closed). FOURTH and last new-endpoint slice in v5 family. Identical wire shape vs v3; only divergence is DELETE /{id} → 204 (v3: 200 empty). No bulk endpoints in either version. ~105 LOC source + ~125 LOC tests. 4 type aliases + 5 re-exported handlers + 1 new DeleteHandler. 6 tests (4 on DELETE behaviour + 1 smoke + parseID helper). 6 routes wired at `/api/v5/autotagging*`. **All four new-endpoint v5 families (b/c/d/e) closed; C.1 backlog has only C.1.i left.**

- **J224** — v5 ImportList Settings controller port (audit row C.1.d closed). Third v5-endpoint-family slice. Smallest possible — pure path-alias. New `internal/api/v5/importlistsettings/` package: 3 type aliases (Resource / Getter / Writer from v3) + 2 re-exported handler vars (GetHandler / UpdateHandler). The C# v5 SettingsController produces byte-identical status codes (200 / 202) to v3's ImportListConfigController; reflection-based `SaveConfigDictionary` vs v3's explicit setters is a C# host implementation detail with no on-wire effect. 3 routes wired at `/api/v5/settings/importlist*`. 4 tests including the KeepAndTag + tag=0 validation pin.

- **J223** — v5 ImportList controller port (audit row C.1.c closed). Second v5-endpoint-family slice. New `internal/api/v5/importlist/` package (~225 LOC source + ~310 LOC tests). Type-aliases Resource + BulkResource + 6 dependency interfaces (Lister/ByIDGetter/Writer/Emitter/Tester/Actioner) from v3; re-exports 7 handlers (List/ByID/Schema/Create/Update/TestAll/RequestAction); ships 4 NEW handlers (Delete → 204, Test → 204, BulkUpdate → 200, BulkDelete → 204) using a response-rewriting middleware approach — `rewriteSuccessTo204` (predicate-gated: only `{}` body triggers downgrade, strips Content-Type) + `rewrite202To200` (status-only demote, body preserved). 12-LOC `bufferingWriter` (minimal ResponseWriter recorder) buffers v3's response so the wrapper can decide what to forward. Pattern advantage over J222's duplicate-and-modify: v5 doesn't fork SignalR broadcast logic / validation chain / body parsing — all that stays in v3's tested handlers. Trade-off: an extra in-memory buffer per v5 request (acceptable — these handlers are CRUD, not hot paths). 11 routes wired alongside v3. 14 tests including 4 middleware-predicate pins.

- **J222** — v5 CustomFormat controller port (audit row C.1.b closed). New `internal/api/v5/customformat/` package (~210 LOC source + ~250 LOC tests). Resource + BulkResource type-aliased from v3 (wire shape unchanged); 5 handlers re-exported (GET list / GET by-id / POST / PUT /{id} / GET schema — same status codes); 3 new handlers (DeleteHandler → 204, BulkUpdateHandler → 200, BulkDeleteHandler → 204 empty) implement the v5 `TypedResults` status-code tightening. 8 new routes at `/api/v5/customformat*` wired alongside the v3 routes. Two helper exports added to v3 (`CustomFormatModelFromFormat`, `ToCustomFormatResource`) for cross-version reuse. 14 new tests covering all three v5-specific status codes + happy path + 404 + 400 + null-coalesce-preserves-existing semantics. First C.1.b–e new-endpoint-family slice; sets the pattern for C.1.c (ImportList) which is structurally identical.

- **J221** — v5-develop catch-up merge (audit row C.1.a closed). 58 upstream commits (277 files / +10839 / -5639) absorbed in a single merge commit; only conflict was `global.json` (.NET SDK pin — combined v5-develop's bumped `10.0.300` with go-port's existing `rollForward: latestMinor` relaxation). Zero Go-side conflicts. Hard gates all clean (build / vet / test / lint — lint surfaces 7 pre-existing baseline issues, identical pre- and post-merge). Localization refresh + golden re-record deferred to C.1.i. New v5 endpoint families (`src/Sonarr.Api.V5/CustomFormats/{Controller,BulkResource}.cs`, `ImportLists/{Controller,Resource,BulkResource}.cs`, `Settings/ImportListSettings{Controller,Resource}.cs`, `AutoTagging/{Controller,Resource,SpecificationSchema}.cs`) now in tree as port sources for C.1.b–e. Fifth standalone C.1 closure this loop-run after J217/J218/J219/J220.

- **J220** — Localhost auth bypass port (audit row C.1.g closed; upstream commit `0ea943cdb`). Adds `DisabledForLocalhost` (=2) to the `AuthenticationRequiredType` enum surface (`internal/api/v3/enum_naming.go` — new camelCase→PascalCase case); extends `host.allowLocalBypass` (`internal/host/auth.go`) with a strict-loopback bypass branch matching C# `UiAuthorizationHandler.cs:39-47` (no LAN / link-local / CGNAT extensions). Mode-string match remains `strings.EqualFold`. 9 new tests (1 enum reversal + 8 middleware cases). Localization key + frontend dropdown deferred to C.1.a merge per the J217/J218/J219 standalone-forward-port pattern (golden-byte-eq prevents drifting `internal/localization/en.json` solo). Fourth standalone C.1 closure this loop-run.

- **J219** — Pushover end-to-end encryption port (audit row C.1.f closed; upstream commit `e79077d5f`). New `EncryptionKey string` field on `pushover.Settings` mirroring C# `PushoverSettings.cs:57`. New `pushover/encryption.go` ports the C# `EncryptField` / `GzipCompress` / `HexStringToBytes` / `validateEncryptionKey` helpers from `PushoverProxy.cs:97-156`: AES-256-CBC + PKCS#7 padding + random IV + HMAC-SHA256 authentication + gzip compression, base64-encoded envelope. `sendNotification` modified to encrypt title + message when EncryptionKey is set + add `encrypted=1` to the form. Plaintext path preserved when key is empty. Settings validator added with the same regex (`^[0-9a-fA-F]{64}$`) C# uses. 14 new tests: 4 settings-validator branches (empty / valid / 4 invalid patterns) + helper tests for hexStringToBytes / pkcs7Pad full-block + partial-block padding + critical EncryptField_RoundTripsCleanly (5 plaintexts: ASCII / empty / 1KB-compressible / multi-line / UTF-8) that inverts the envelope via the same crypto primitives Pushover's client uses + IV uniqueness test + wrong-key-length error + 2 integration tests through OnGrab (encrypted-flag + ciphertext when key set; plaintext wire when key absent) + 1 settings-integration test for the EncryptionKey rejection path.
- **J218** — Audit row C.1.h CLOSED (upstream commit `5477cd262` — "Add CancellationToken to HttpClient async methods"). Pure verification slice: the C# upstream added `CancellationToken` params to the entire `IHttpClient` async surface; the Go-port already uses `context.Context` end-to-end via `http.NewRequestWithContext` at the dispatcher seam (`httpclient/dispatcher.go:233`). Whole-tree grep for `http.NewRequest` outside of `WithContext` returns zero matches. No production-code change required — the Go-port's architecture was already aligned from day 1. Added `TestDispatcher_ContextCancellation_PropagatesToHTTPClient` to `httpclient/dispatcher_test.go` as a durable regression guard: server delays response, client cancels via ctx, Dispatch must return `context.Canceled`. A future refactor that swaps `NewRequestWithContext` for `NewRequest` will fail this test.
- **J217** — Forward-port of upstream C# commit `b81de8570` (audit row C.1.j — "Recently-aired refresh logging"). Adds the info-log line at C# `EpisodeRefreshedService.cs:47` ("Searching for {MissingCount} episodes from '{SeriesTitle}' that were recently added or had absolute episode number added") to the Go-port's `episoderefreshed.Service.Search`. C# upstream changed the Search signature from `Search(int seriesId)` to `Search(Series series)` so the new log could carry the series title — Go-port preserves the by-id signature and loads the series via the existing SeriesLookup dep internally. Series-lookup error absorption added: a GetSeries failure on the title fetch degrades to a less-informative log line + the dispatch still runs (matches the established warn-and-continue posture from J191). Independent of C.1.a v5 merge — one self-contained behaviour ported standalone. 1 new test: `Search_SeriesLookupErr_AbsorbedDispatchStillRuns` verifies the absorption + dispatch still fires.
- **J216** — SeriesScannedHandler post-add-actions arm port (C# `Tv/SeriesScannedHandler.cs:36-74` AddOptions!=null branch — the partner to the J211 AddOptions==null partial port). New `internal/seriesadd.Service` with `HandleSeriesScanned` + `HandleSeriesScanSkipped` subscribers running the 4-step C# sequence: (1) `episodemonitored.Service.SetEpisodeMonitoredStatus` cascade per AddOptions.Monitor; (2) publish new `series.SeriesAddCompletedEvent`; (3) push search command based on AddOptions search flags (`SeriesSearch` when both flags / `MissingEpisodeSearch` when only-Missing / DEFERRED when only-CutoffUnmet since handler not yet ported); (4) `series.Service.RemoveAddOptions` to clear the post-add gate. All step errors absorbed via warn-log + continue so the post-add lifecycle completes as far as possible. 4 narrow interfaces (MonitorCascader / CommandPusher / AddOptionsClearer + optional bus); each subscriber gates the opposite way on AddOptions from the J211 episoderefreshed subscribers, so the two paths never double-handle. Wired at main.go next to the J211 subscribers; constructs a separate episodemonitored.Service instance for the cascade (the J214 instance lives in host_wiring.go scope). 14 tests covering: 3 constructor nil-dep validation + 3 AddOptions gate paths (nil-series / no-AddOptions / malformed-AddOptions) + 4 search-flag branches (both / only-missing / only-cutoff-deferred / neither) + 3 error-absorption tests + 1 scan-skipped routing test + 1 SeriesAddCompletedEvent publish test (uses real messaging.Bus with subscriber). **The full SeriesScannedHandler is now ported end-to-end (J211 + J216 cover both AddOptions branches).**

- **J215** — `SeriesSearchCommand` handler port (C# `IndexerSearch/SeriesSearchService.cs:33-75`). New `internal/commands/handlers/seriessearch.go` with `Execute(ctx, body) error` dispatching on two branches: no monitored seasons → per-episode mode (filter monitored+missing+aired, per-episode dispatch); ≥1 monitored season → per-season mode (sort by SeasonNumber ASC, skip unmonitored, per-season dispatch). 4 narrow optional interfaces (SeriesSearchLookup, SeriesSearchEpisodesLister, EpisodeSearcher reused from J39, SeasonSearcher new). Documented divergences: quality-profile `UpgradeAllowed` → `missingOnly` hard-coded false (matches C# default profile); Grabbed-count tracking deferred (matches J39 — counts decisions). Wired into commands/handlers/Deps + RegisterAll; main.go gets new `seasonSearchAdapter` wrapping `*indexersearch.Service.SeasonSearch` into the decisions-count shape, alongside the existing per-episode `handlerEpisodeSearchAdapter` (J39). 9 tests covering nil-deps stub + body validation + series-not-found + per-episode mode filter predicate (5 edge cases) + per-season ASC ordering + skip-unmonitored + per-episode error absorb + per-season error absorb + empty-Seasons-fallthrough-to-per-episode. Closes the second dependency the SeriesScannedHandler post-add-actions arm needs (after J213 closed EpisodeMonitoredService); remaining: SeriesAddCompletedEvent type definition.

Sequencing: C.1.a merge first, then small warm-ups (C.1.h CancellationToken parity check, C.1.j refresh-log line), then single-area behaviour ports (C.1.f Pushover E2E, C.1.g Localhost auth bypass), then new v5 endpoint family (C.1.b CustomFormat → C.1.c ImportList → C.1.d ImportList Settings → C.1.e AutoTagging), then C.1.i contract-replay re-baseline. **Per-slice protocol: one sub-item = one slice.**

Mirrors the MissingEpisodeSearch handler structure (J39) with a different filter predicate (file present BUT quality below cutoff). Needs a quality-cutoff comparator from qualityprofiles. Once landed the post-add-actions arm fires the right command in all 4 search-flag branches.

6. **Windows-only GetActualCasing impl** (~150 LOC) — split `pathcasing.go` into `pathcasing_windows.go` + `pathcasing_other.go` and implement the parent-walk casing recovery via Win32 FindFirstFileW. Low priority (Sonarr containers run Linux); useful for native Windows installers.
